03Inventory, Safety Stock, and Service LevelsPlenty of inventory, none of these items available+

Your goal

Explain item-level availability and service measures without treating a high total balance or safety-stock policy as proof of fulfillment.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

Plenty of inventory, none of these items available

Inventory planner Elena and distribution manager Omar review a warehouse with a total inventory value of two million dollars. Item A has 40 units on hand, all allocated to existing orders. Item B has 80 units on hand, all blocked by a quality hold. New orders for both items remain unfilled. No available substitute or confirmed replenishment date is supplied. Elena must explain why the headline value does not resolve these shortages and distinguish physical stock from stock available to promise.

Elena: Inventory planner / Omar: Distribution manager

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many A units are available to the new orders under the supplied facts?

Answer and explanation

A. Zero, because all 40 are allocated

The forty A units are already assigned, and no reallocation is authorized in the brief.

2. How many B units are currently available under the stated quality status?

Answer and explanation

B. Zero, because all 80 are blocked

The quality hold blocks all eighty B units; demand does not supply release authorization.

3. Why does the total value not resolve the shortages?

Answer and explanation

A. Value aggregates different items and states and does not establish available A or B units.

A high aggregate value can coexist with no available stock for specific requested items.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Physical presence does not establish free availability. All 40 A units are allocated; all 80 B units are blocked. Neither group is available for the new orders on the supplied facts. A total dollar value cannot be substituted for an item quantity.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
stock-keeping unit
A distinct inventory item identifier, commonly abbreviated SKU.analyze each stock-keeping unit
on-hand inventory
The quantity physically recorded at the stated location.reconcile on-hand inventory
available inventory
Stock usable for the specified demand after relevant restrictions.confirm available inventory
allocated stock
Stock assigned to particular demand or orders.review allocated stock
blocked stock
Stock restricted from use under a defined status.identify blocked stock
quality hold
A restriction pending the required quality review or decision.respect a quality hold
available to promise
The quantity that can be committed under the defined supply-and-demand rules.calculate available to promise
inventory position
A defined balance considering on-hand stock, incoming supply, and demand commitments.state the inventory-position basis
safety stock
Buffer inventory intended to protect against specified uncertainty.review safety-stock assumptions
cycle stock
Inventory supporting normal demand between replenishments.distinguish cycle stock
reorder point
The inventory level or position that triggers replenishment under a policy.review the reorder point
replenishment lead time
The elapsed time from a defined replenishment trigger to usable receipt.measure replenishment lead time
stockout
Inability to supply an item when required from the relevant inventory.investigate stockouts
fill rate
The share of defined demand filled under the stated service measure.define the fill-rate basis
cycle service level
The probability of avoiding a stockout during a defined replenishment cycle.distinguish cycle service level
backorder
Demand retained for later fulfillment after it cannot be supplied as required.track backorders
inventory turnover
A period's inventory usage or cost relative to the defined average inventory.interpret inventory turnover
days of supply
Inventory expressed as coverage of demand at a stated rate.calculate days of supply
slow-moving stock
Inventory used or sold less frequently than the defined threshold.identify slow-moving stock
obsolete stock
Inventory no longer suitable or expected to meet its intended demand.assess obsolete stock
ABC analysis
Grouping items by a selected importance measure, often annual consumption value.perform ABC analysis
item segmentation
Grouping items by relevant demand, supply, or service characteristics.refine item segmentation
inventory accuracy
Agreement between recorded and actual inventory under the defined measure.improve inventory accuracy
reallocation
Authorized reassignment of stock from one demand to another.assess a reallocation request

Safety stock is a buffer against defined uncertainty, not a guarantee against every stockout. Fill rate and cycle service level measure different things. Name the demand unit, time period, location, restrictions, and service definition before comparing percentages.

C Notice the language

Answer the question at the right level

A manager may ask how shortages are possible when inventory is high. Start by separating value, item identity, location, and usability. This turns a seeming contradiction into a concrete allocation and replenishment discussion without implying that all existing stock is useless or every policy has failed.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Start with the distinction
The value total does not show availability for these two items.
State A's position
All 40 A units are allocated to existing orders.
State B's restriction
All 80 B units remain blocked by the quality hold.
Keep available quantity explicit
Neither item has available stock for these new orders on the current facts.
Avoid an unauthorized release
New demand does not remove the quality restriction.
Avoid double-promising
We cannot promise the same allocated stock to two orders.
Request item-level detail
Show item, location, quantity, allocation, and status.
Clarify incoming supply
A planned receipt is not yet usable on-hand inventory.
Name the service measure
Are we measuring units filled, complete orders, or stockout-free cycles?
Keep the buffer realistic
Safety stock reduces exposure to specified uncertainty; it is not a universal guarantee.
Check policy inputs
Review demand variability and replenishment assumptions for the affected items.
Separate slow stock
High value in another item does not supply a missing unit of A.
Define any reallocation
A priority change needs an authorized allocation decision.
Avoid a premature policy fix
A larger buffer proposal needs an item-specific basis.
State the missing date
No confirmed replenishment date is supplied.
Close the explanation
Report the headline value and the item-level shortage together.
6 language and precision notes
On hand
Describes recorded physical quantity, not necessarily free usable quantity.
Available
Must specify the demand, location, and restrictions.
Allocated
Assigned already; do not silently promise it again.
Blocked
A controlled restriction, not a suggestion to ignore when demand rises.
Service level
An umbrella phrase; identify the actual measure.
Coverage
Depends on the chosen demand rate and the usable stock basis.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which management summary is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. Inventory value is high, but A is fully allocated and B fully blocked, leaving no available stock for the new orders.

The summary distinguishes the aggregate value from the specific allocation and quality restrictions.

2. Another item has 60 on hand, 35 allocated, and 10 blocked, with no overlap. What remains free?

Answer and explanation

D. 15 units

Subtracting the separate allocated and blocked quantities from sixty leaves fifteen units.

3. Which claim about safety stock is defensible?

Answer and explanation

A. It buffers specified uncertainty but does not guarantee that every item will always be available.

A buffer depends on assumptions and cannot override status restrictions or every demand and supply outcome.

4. A later report shows 95% unit fill rate. Which conclusion needs a separate measure?

Answer and explanation

D. Ninety-five percent of replenishment cycles had no stockout.

A unit-based fill rate and a stockout-free cycle measure use different denominators; one percentage cannot establish the other.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

Plenty of inventory, none of these items available

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Elena: Inventory planner / Omar: Distribution manager

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • quality hold
  • cycle service level
  • reallocation
  • allocated stock
  • stock-keeping unit
  • available to promise
  • fill rate
  • On-hand inventory
  • replenishment lead time
  • safety stock
  1. Omar
    We have two million dollars of inventory, yet new orders for A and B are unfilled. The total looks healthy, so I need a clear explanation.
  2. Elena
    Start at the 1 level. Value across many items does not establish that the specific products requested are available in the right state and location.
  3. Omar
    The record shows forty units of A physically here. Why can those not satisfy the new demand if the items are already in the building?
  4. Elena
    They are 2. All forty belong to existing orders under the current allocation; promising them again would create two commitments against the same quantity.
  5. Omar
    Can we display the allocation beside the forty-unit count? At the moment, the first screen makes those units look available to anyone taking a new order.
  6. Elena
    Exactly. 3 and available inventory are different views. The same distinction matters for B, although its restriction comes from quality status rather than allocation.
  7. Omar
    B has eighty units on hand, but the status is blocked. I assume the new order does not give distribution authority to remove that status.
  8. Elena
    Correct. The 4 remains in force. We cannot treat the eighty units as usable merely because they have value or are physically present.
  9. Omar
    So neither item has free stock for the new orders on the current facts. That does not mean the whole warehouse is empty or all our stock is obsolete.
  10. Elena
    Right. Our 5 assessment must respect allocations and restrictions. We also have no confirmed replenishment date or available substitute in this briefing.
  11. Omar
    Should we simply increase safety stock for both items? The repeated shortages suggest a problem, but I do not know whether a larger buffer is the appropriate response.
  12. Elena
    Review the 6 assumptions rather than assume the answer. Demand variation, supply timing, and the reason for unavailable stock need item-specific examination.
  13. Omar
    Lead-time assumptions may also matter. A receipt that arrives later than expected could undermine the intended buffer, even if the original quantity calculation looked reasonable.
  14. Elena
    Yes. Use the actual 7 definition, including when stock becomes usable. A shipment in transit is not the same as inventory available at the required location.
  15. Omar
    Our service dashboard says ninety-five percent, but the label does not say whether that means units supplied or orders supplied completely. That makes comparisons difficult.
  16. Elena
    Define the 8 basis explicitly. Units, order lines, and complete orders can produce different percentages, and none should be silently substituted for the others.
  17. Omar
    The replenishment report counts cycles with no shortage. Should that have a separate label so our teams stop comparing it directly with the demand-fill figure?
  18. Elena
    That is 9. It is not automatically equal to a demand-fill percentage, so a shared label of service level can conceal an important difference.
  19. Omar
    I will explain the two-million-dollar total alongside the item-specific restrictions. Any proposal to move A from existing orders will also need a clear priority decision.
  20. Elena
    Yes, any 10 must be authorized and visible. For now, report A fully allocated, B fully blocked, and replenishment unconfirmed rather than imply the headline value resolves the shortages.

Answers and explanations
  1. stock-keeping unit Turn 2. A stock-keeping unit identifies the particular item, which the aggregate dollar balance does not distinguish.
  2. allocated stock Turn 4. The forty A units are already assigned to existing orders, so they are not free for the new demand.
  3. On-hand inventory Turn 6. On-hand inventory records physical quantity but does not by itself account for allocation or other use restrictions.
  4. quality hold Turn 8. The quality hold blocks B from use pending the required decision and is not removed by customer demand.
  5. available to promise Turn 10. Available to promise concerns what can be committed under the relevant supply and demand rules, not the gross physical balance.
  6. safety stock Turn 12. Safety stock is a buffer based on uncertainty assumptions, not an automatic cure for allocations, holds, or every shortage.
  7. replenishment lead time Turn 14. Replenishment lead time includes the defined interval to usable receipt, which can affect inventory-policy assumptions.
  8. fill rate Turn 16. Fill rate measures fulfilled demand under a specified unit and timing definition, so the basis must be stated.
  9. cycle service level Turn 18. Cycle service level concerns avoiding stockouts during replenishment cycles rather than the share of demand units filled.
  10. reallocation Turn 20. Reallocation changes which demand receives stock and requires an authorized decision rather than a second promise of the same units.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Omar: We have two million dollars of inventory, yet new orders for A and B are unfilled. The total looks healthy, so I need a clear explanation.
  2. Elena: Start at the stock-keeping unit level. Value across many items does not establish that the specific products requested are available in the right state and location.
  3. Omar: The record shows forty units of A physically here. Why can those not satisfy the new demand if the items are already in the building?
  4. Elena: They are allocated stock. All forty belong to existing orders under the current allocation; promising them again would create two commitments against the same quantity.
  5. Omar: Can we display the allocation beside the forty-unit count? At the moment, the first screen makes those units look available to anyone taking a new order.
  6. Elena: Exactly. On-hand inventory and available inventory are different views. The same distinction matters for B, although its restriction comes from quality status rather than allocation.
  7. Omar: B has eighty units on hand, but the status is blocked. I assume the new order does not give distribution authority to remove that status.
  8. Elena: Correct. The quality hold remains in force. We cannot treat the eighty units as usable merely because they have value or are physically present.
  9. Omar: So neither item has free stock for the new orders on the current facts. That does not mean the whole warehouse is empty or all our stock is obsolete.
  10. Elena: Right. Our available to promise assessment must respect allocations and restrictions. We also have no confirmed replenishment date or available substitute in this briefing.
  11. Omar: Should we simply increase safety stock for both items? The repeated shortages suggest a problem, but I do not know whether a larger buffer is the appropriate response.
  12. Elena: Review the safety stock assumptions rather than assume the answer. Demand variation, supply timing, and the reason for unavailable stock need item-specific examination.
  13. Omar: Lead-time assumptions may also matter. A receipt that arrives later than expected could undermine the intended buffer, even if the original quantity calculation looked reasonable.
  14. Elena: Yes. Use the actual replenishment lead time definition, including when stock becomes usable. A shipment in transit is not the same as inventory available at the required location.
  15. Omar: Our service dashboard says ninety-five percent, but the label does not say whether that means units supplied or orders supplied completely. That makes comparisons difficult.
  16. Elena: Define the fill rate basis explicitly. Units, order lines, and complete orders can produce different percentages, and none should be silently substituted for the others.
  17. Omar: The replenishment report counts cycles with no shortage. Should that have a separate label so our teams stop comparing it directly with the demand-fill figure?
  18. Elena: That is cycle service level. It is not automatically equal to a demand-fill percentage, so a shared label of service level can conceal an important difference.
  19. Omar: I will explain the two-million-dollar total alongside the item-specific restrictions. Any proposal to move A from existing orders will also need a clear priority decision.
  20. Elena: Yes, any reallocation must be authorized and visible. For now, report A fully allocated, B fully blocked, and replenishment unconfirmed rather than imply the headline value resolves the shortages.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

High total stock with two recurring stockouts

Fictional inventory review: total inventory is high, but two important SKUs repeatedly stock out; item-level demand and stock policies need review.

  1. Operations manager: Our total inventory looks healthy. Why are two important items still experiencing stockouts?
  2. Inventory planner: The total combines different SKUs; stock of one item doesn't necessarily satisfy demand for another.
  3. Operations manager: So a large aggregate can hide the wrong mix of inventory across the product range?
  4. Inventory planner: Exactly. We need item-level availability and demand, not only the total value or quantity.
  5. Operations manager: Would increasing safety stock everywhere automatically solve the service-level problem?
  6. Inventory planner: Not necessarily. We should assess the affected items and their replenishment conditions before proposing changes.
  7. Operations manager: Can you explain SKU for the newer colleague joining this review?
  8. Inventory planner: It means stock-keeping unit, the identifier for a particular stocked item in our system.
  9. Operations manager: I'll ask for separate results for the two items rather than treat the headline as reassurance.
  10. Inventory planner: I'll bring the verified item-level picture and keep any policy recommendation tied to the actual evidence.
On-hand stock versus stock available to promise

Separate fictional inventory case: SKU Q5 has 100 units on hand, of which 70 are already allocated; no other holds or receipts are included.

  1. Customer service lead: Q5 shows one hundred units on hand. Can I promise all one hundred to this new customer?
  2. Inventory controller: Seventy are already allocated, leaving thirty unallocated in this simplified case.
  3. Customer service lead: So on-hand quantity and quantity available for a new promise are different measures?
  4. Inventory controller: Yes. We must account for existing commitments instead of treating the same units as available twice.
  5. Customer service lead: Could safety stock be counted again as an extra pool outside the one hundred?
  6. Inventory controller: Not without the actual stock definition. Safety stock is not automatically additional physical inventory beyond that count.
  7. Customer service lead: I'll explain that thirty are unallocated here, while any customer commitment still follows our order process.
  8. Inventory controller: Good. Don't invent future receipts to make the requested quantity fit.
  9. Customer service lead: I'll check the customer's required quantity and timing before offering a confirmed option.
  10. Inventory controller: I'll verify the current allocation status so the final promise uses the right SKU and stock basis.
A service percentage needs its denominator

Separate fictional service review: nine of ten orders shipped complete, but those nine contained only half of the units ordered in total.

  1. Sales manager: Nine out of ten orders shipped complete. Can I say we fulfilled ninety percent of all units?
  2. Supply analyst: No. That is an order-based measure; the nine complete orders contained only half of the units ordered.
  3. Sales manager: Then the same fulfillment performance can look different when measured by orders rather than units?
  4. Supply analyst: Correct. Each measure answers a different service question, so the denominator must be named.
  5. Sales manager: Does the remaining order automatically count as a total stockout for every SKU it contains?
  6. Supply analyst: We don't have that detail. Don't infer item-level availability from the aggregate order status.
  7. Sales manager: I'll describe the ninety percent as complete-order performance, not the share of units supplied.
  8. Supply analyst: We need the quantity shipped on the incomplete order before calculating unit fulfillment.
  9. Sales manager: I'll ask for the item breakdown before discussing safety-stock changes with the team.
  10. Supply analyst: Good. A precise service-level definition helps us identify the actual problem without turning one percentage into a universal success claim.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Plenty of inventory, none of these items available
  1. Read the corrected dialogue, contrasting physically present with available for new orders for both A and B.
  2. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Keep fill rate, cycle service level, and an authorized reallocation distinct.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Free quantity has a status basis

An item has 100 units on hand. Sixty are allocated and 25 are blocked, with no overlap. No receipt is confirmed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Planner: "The physical on-hand quantity is one ___."

Answer and explanation

D. hundred

The physical record contains 100 units before restrictions are deducted.

2. Manager: "The allocated quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

A. sixty

Sixty units are assigned to existing demand in the supplied facts.

3. Planner: "The remaining free quantity is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. fifteen

100 minus the separate 60 allocated and 25 blocked units leaves fifteen.

4. Manager: "Future replenishment remains ___."

Answer and explanation

D. unconfirmed

The scenario supplies no confirmed incoming receipt or replenishment date.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Lesson 03: Inventory, Safety Stock, and Service Levels
Original fictional case: Inventory planner Elena and distribution manager Omar review a warehouse with a total inventory value of two million dollars. Item A has 40 units on hand, all allocated to existing orders. Item B has 80 units on hand, all blocked by a quality hold. New orders for both items remain unfilled. No available substitute or confirmed replenishment date is supplied. Elena must explain why the headline value does not resolve these shortages and distinguish physical stock from stock available to promise.
Language workshop: Plenty of inventory, none of these items available
Communication goal: Explain item-level availability and service measures without treating a high total balance or safety-stock policy as proof of fulfillment.
Language guidance: Physical presence does not establish free availability. All 40 A units are allocated; all 80 B units are blocked. Neither group is available for the new orders on the supplied facts. A total dollar value cannot be substituted for an item quantity.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The value total does not show availability for these two items. / All 40 A units are allocated to existing orders. / All 80 B units remain blocked by the quality hold. / Neither item has available stock for these new orders on the current facts. / New demand does not remove the quality restriction. / We cannot promise the same allocated stock to two orders. / Show item, location, quantity, allocation, and status. / A planned receipt is not yet usable on-hand inventory. / Are we measuring units filled, complete orders, or stockout-free cycles? / Safety stock reduces exposure to specified uncertainty; it is not a universal guarantee. / Review demand variability and replenishment assumptions for the affected items. / High value in another item does not supply a missing unit of A. / A priority change needs an authorized allocation decision. / A larger buffer proposal needs an item-specific basis. / No confirmed replenishment date is supplied. / Report the headline value and the item-level shortage together.
Vocabulary: stock-keeping unit: A distinct inventory item identifier, commonly abbreviated SKU. / on-hand inventory: The quantity physically recorded at the stated location. / available inventory: Stock usable for the specified demand after relevant restrictions. / allocated stock: Stock assigned to particular demand or orders. / blocked stock: Stock restricted from use under a defined status. / quality hold: A restriction pending the required quality review or decision. / available to promise: The quantity that can be committed under the defined supply-and-demand rules. / inventory position: A defined balance considering on-hand stock, incoming supply, and demand commitments. / safety stock: Buffer inventory intended to protect against specified uncertainty. / cycle stock: Inventory supporting normal demand between replenishments. / reorder point: The inventory level or position that triggers replenishment under a policy. / replenishment lead time: The elapsed time from a defined replenishment trigger to usable receipt. / stockout: Inability to supply an item when required from the relevant inventory. / fill rate: The share of defined demand filled under the stated service measure. / cycle service level: The probability of avoiding a stockout during a defined replenishment cycle. / backorder: Demand retained for later fulfillment after it cannot be supplied as required. / inventory turnover: A period's inventory usage or cost relative to the defined average inventory. / days of supply: Inventory expressed as coverage of demand at a stated rate. / slow-moving stock: Inventory used or sold less frequently than the defined threshold. / obsolete stock: Inventory no longer suitable or expected to meet its intended demand. / ABC analysis: Grouping items by a selected importance measure, often annual consumption value. / item segmentation: Grouping items by relevant demand, supply, or service characteristics. / inventory accuracy: Agreement between recorded and actual inventory under the defined measure. / reallocation: Authorized reassignment of stock from one demand to another.
Speaking task: Read the corrected dialogue, contrasting physically present with available for new orders for both A and B. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Keep fill rate, cycle service level, and an authorized reallocation distinct. Complete and check the transfer. Say the quantities in order: 100 on hand, 60 allocated, 25 separately blocked, 15 free.
Partner: Elena: Inventory planner / Omar: Distribution manager
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: An item has 100 units on hand. Sixty are allocated and 25 are blocked, with no overlap. No receipt is confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Omar: The replenishment report counts cycles with no shortage. Should that have a separate label so our teams stop comparing it directly with the demand-fill figure?
Elena: That is cycle service level. It is not automatically equal to a demand-fill percentage, so a shared label of service level can conceal an important difference.
Omar: I will explain the two-million-dollar total alongside the item-specific restrictions. Any proposal to move A from existing orders will also need a clear priority decision.
Elena: Yes, any reallocation must be authorized and visible. For now, report A fully allocated, B fully blocked, and replenishment unconfirmed rather than imply the headline value resolves the shortages.
END REFERENCE

3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

/ quality hold: A restriction pending the required quality review or decision. / available to promise: The quantity that can be committed under the defined supply-and-demand rules. / inventory position: A defined balance considering on-hand stock, incoming supply, and demand commitments. / safety stock: Buffer inventory intended to protect against specified uncertainty. / cycle stock: Inventory supporting normal demand between replenishments. / reorder point: The inventory level or position that triggers replenishment under a policy. / replenishment lead time: The elapsed time from a defined replenishment trigger to usable receipt. / stockout: Inability to supply an item when required from the relevant inventory. / fill rate: The share of defined demand filled under the stated service measure. / cycle service level: The probability of avoiding a stockout during a defined replenishment cycle. / backorder: Demand retained for later fulfillment after it cannot be supplied as required. / inventory turnover: A period's inventory usage or cost relative to the defined average inventory. / days of supply: Inventory expressed as coverage of demand at a stated rate. / slow-moving stock: Inventory used or sold less frequently than the defined threshold. / obsolete stock: Inventory no longer suitable or expected to meet its intended demand. / ABC analysis: Grouping items by a selected importance measure, often annual consumption value. / item segmentation: Grouping items by relevant demand, supply, or service characteristics. / inventory accuracy: Agreement between recorded and actual inventory under the defined measure. / reallocation: Authorized reassignment of stock from one demand to another.
Speaking task: Read the corrected dialogue, contrasting physically present with available for new orders for both A and B. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Keep fill rate, cycle service level, and an authorized reallocation distinct. Complete and check the transfer. Say the quantities in order: 100 on hand, 60 allocated, 25 separately blocked, 15 free.
Partner: Elena: Inventory planner / Omar: Distribution manager
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: An item has 100 units on hand. Sixty are allocated and 25 are blocked, with no overlap. No receipt is confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Omar: The replenishment report counts cycles with no shortage. Should that have a separate label so our teams stop comparing it directly with the demand-fill figure?
Elena: That is cycle service level. It is not automatically equal to a demand-fill percentage, so a shared label of service level can conceal an important difference.
Omar: I will explain the two-million-dollar total alongside the item-specific restrictions. Any proposal to move A from existing orders will also need a clear priority decision.
Elena: Yes, any reallocation must be authorized and visible. For now, report A fully allocated, B fully blocked, and replenishment unconfirmed rather than imply the headline value resolves the shortages.
END REFERENCE
04Warehousing and FulfillmentShipments and the closing queue+

Your goal

Reconcile a changing order queue, distinguish gross completions from net reduction, and avoid unsupported claims about order age or service.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Shipments and the closing queue

Warehouse lead Pavel and customer-operations manager Zoe review a shift that began with 300 open orders. Fifty new orders arrived during the shift, and 220 orders were fully shipped and closed. There were no cancellations, reopenings, or partial closures. The data do not identify which shipped orders came from the opening queue. A report says 80 remain and the oldest orders are cleared. Pavel and Zoe must correct the balance and separate the known throughput from the unsupported age and service claims.

Pavel: Warehouse lead / Zoe: Customer-operations manager

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many orders remain open?

Answer and explanation

A. 130

Opening 300 plus 50 arrivals minus 220 completed orders equals 130 open orders.

2. What is the net reduction from the opening queue total?

Answer and explanation

C. 170 orders

The open total falls from 300 to 130, a net reduction of 170 after new arrivals.

3. What is unknown about the shipped orders?

Answer and explanation

C. Which were from the opening queue

The brief explicitly leaves the cohort identity of shipped orders unspecified.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

300 opening orders plus 50 arrivals minus 220 completed orders equals 130 closing orders. Gross completions are 220; the net queue reduction is 170. Ignoring arrivals produces the incorrect balance of 80.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
order backlog
Orders still awaiting the defined fulfillment completion.reconcile the order backlog
opening balance
The quantity at the start of the reporting period.state the opening balance
closing balance
The quantity at the end after all relevant movements.calculate the closing balance
order inflow
New orders entering the queue during a period.track order inflow
gross completions
Total completed orders before accounting for new arrivals or other queue changes.report gross completions
net reduction
The decrease in the total balance after additions and removals.distinguish net reduction
fulfillment cycle
The steps from accepted order through the defined delivery or completion point.map the fulfillment cycle
order release
Authorization to begin warehouse processing for an order.schedule order release
wave picking
Grouping orders into planned picking batches or time windows.organize wave picking
pick face
The location from which items are selected for orders.replenish the pick face
pick accuracy
The correctness of selected items and quantities under the defined measure.monitor pick accuracy
short pick
A pick that cannot supply the full requested quantity.investigate a short pick
packing verification
Checking packed items against order and shipping requirements.complete packing verification
shipment confirmation
A recorded confirmation of the defined dispatch event.verify shipment confirmation
partial fulfillment
Completion of only part of an order.flag partial fulfillment
order aging
The time orders have remained open under the defined starting point.review order aging
due-date priority
Ranking work by agreed completion or delivery deadlines.assess due-date priority
cutoff time
The latest time for inclusion in a specified processing or dispatch cycle.confirm the cutoff time
warehouse management system
Software managing warehouse inventory and operations, abbreviated WMS.reconcile the warehouse management system
scan event
A recorded identification action at a process step.trace scan events
exception queue
Work set aside for problems requiring resolution.review the exception queue
dock capacity
The available capability for loading, unloading, and related dock work.assess dock capacity
labor capacity
The work volume supported by available personnel under stated conditions.review labor capacity
order cohort
A defined group of orders, such as those open at the start of a shift.analyze an order cohort

The data do not identify which order cohort shipped. You cannot conclude that all oldest orders were cleared or that 220 of the original 300 shipped. A completed shipment count also does not establish on-time delivery or pick accuracy.

C Notice the language

Reconcile before celebrating

A strong output number deserves recognition, but the closing queue must include new arrivals and other changes. State the arithmetic openly and distinguish volume from customer service. A team can ship many orders while particular older or priority orders still need attention.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Define the starting queue
We began the shift with 300 open orders.
Include arrivals
Fifty new orders entered during the shift.
State completion precisely
Two hundred twenty orders were fully shipped and closed.
Reconcile the ending queue
The closing balance is 130 open orders.
Separate gross and net
We completed 220 orders, but the queue fell by 170.
Explain the correction
Eighty omits the 50 new arrivals.
Keep the cohort open
We do not know which completed orders came from the opening queue.
Avoid an age claim
The total does not prove the oldest orders were cleared.
Request the age view
Show the remaining orders by age and due date.
Separate service measures
Shipped volume does not by itself establish on-time delivery.
Preserve order units
Do not mix orders, order lines, units, and cartons.
Clarify partials
This report counts only fully closed orders.
Check exceptions
Identify unresolved stock, documentation, or address issues separately.
Reconcile the system
Match the completion events to the warehouse record.
Keep the plan conditional
The next-shift plan needs current capacity and priority information.
Close the report
Present arrivals, completions, ending balance, and remaining exceptions together.
6 language and precision notes
Shipped
Define the event; it does not necessarily mean delivered to the customer.
Closed
A system status that needs a clear completion rule.
Remaining
Requires additions as well as removals in the calculation.
Oldest
Refers to order age, which a total alone cannot establish.
Throughput
Volume per stated period, not a synonym for service quality.
Cohort
A defined group whose members must be tracked to make group-level claims.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which closing report is correct?

Answer and explanation

C. 300 opening + 50 new - 220 closed = 130 open

The correct balance includes both the new arrivals and the completed orders.

2. Why is net reduction not 220?

Answer and explanation

A. Fifty new orders offset part of the 220 completions.

The case has fifty additions and no other adjustments, leaving a net reduction of 170.

3. What is the possible number of opening-queue orders still open after this shift?

Answer and explanation

B. Between 80 and 130; the exact count needs order identifiers.

At most 50 of the 220 completions can be new, so 170 to 220 can be opening orders. Subtracting that range from 300 leaves 80 to 130; age priority remains unknown.

4. A later shift starts at 130, receives 40, and fully closes 90, with no other changes. What remains?

Answer and explanation

C. 80 open orders

The balance is 130 plus 40 minus 90, leaving eighty open orders.

E Conversations

Shipments and the closing queue

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Pavel: Warehouse lead / Zoe: Customer-operations manager

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • order cohort
  • partial fulfillment
  • exception queue
  • gross completions
  • closing balance
  • order aging
  • warehouse management system
  • order inflow
  • net reduction
  • shipment confirmation
  1. Zoe
    I am about to send customer service the shift report. It says eighty orders remain. Can you check it before I send the update?
  2. Pavel
    It omits the 1. Fifty new orders arrived after the opening count of three hundred. We also fully shipped and closed two hundred twenty.
  3. Zoe
    Right, I subtracted the shipments but forgot the additions. Three hundred plus fifty, minus two hundred twenty. That leaves one hundred thirty, not eighty.
  4. Pavel
    Yes, that is the 2. There were no cancellations or reopenings, so we do not need another adjustment to reconcile these totals.
  5. Zoe
    Should I reduce the shipment figure to one hundred seventy as well? That is how much smaller the queue is now.
  6. Pavel
    No. Keep two hundred twenty as 3. Those orders really were completed. The extra fifty arrived during the same shift; they do not cancel completed work.
  7. Zoe
    Understood. Two hundred twenty completed, fifty added, and the open queue fell by one hundred seventy. I will label each number separately.
  8. Pavel
    Call the last figure the 4. Otherwise the next shift may think one hundred seventy was our total output rather than the change in unfinished work.
  9. Zoe
    There is another sentence: all the oldest orders are cleared. The completed total is large, but I cannot find the order identifiers behind that claim.
  10. Pavel
    We need the 5 detail. We cannot tell from these totals how many completions belonged to the opening queue or which were new arrivals.
  11. Zoe
    Then I will remove that sentence. Could you send the remaining order ages and due dates before customer service contacts individual customers?
  12. Pavel
    I will pull the 6 report. It can show who is still waiting; the single remaining-order total cannot answer that question.
  13. Zoe
    Include the unresolved address and stock issues separately. Customer service needs to know whether an order is ready to process or waiting on somebody else.
  14. Pavel
    I will add the 7 and its assigned owners. I will not describe those orders as ready until their blocking issues are resolved.
  15. Zoe
    One more point: does fully shipped mean delivered on time? The report heading currently calls these successful deliveries.
  16. Pavel
    No. A 8 records dispatch under our definition. We need the agreed dates and the relevant delivery events before claiming on-time arrival.
  17. Zoe
    I will change that heading too. We are counting fully closed orders, not cartons, individual units, or customer deliveries.
  18. Pavel
    Correct. This count excludes 9. Shipping one carton from an unfinished order does not make that order one of our two hundred twenty closures.
  19. Zoe
    I have corrected eighty to one hundred thirty, kept the completed count, and removed the oldest-cleared and on-time-delivery claims. Please check the underlying events.
  20. Pavel
    I will reconcile them against the 10. Customer service will receive the corrected balance and the age and exception details, with any unresolved differences identified.

Answers and explanations
  1. order inflow Turn 2. The fifty new orders must be added when calculating the closing queue, not omitted from the balance.
  2. closing balance Turn 4. The closing balance is 300 opening plus 50 new minus 220 closed, which equals 130 open orders.
  3. gross completions Turn 6. Gross completions are the 220 fully closed orders, before the queue effect of new arrivals is included.
  4. net reduction Turn 8. Net reduction is the change in the open balance, 300 minus 130, after accounting for additions.
  5. order cohort Turn 10. An order cohort identifies a specific group, and the supplied totals do not show which shipped orders belonged to the opening group.
  6. order aging Turn 12. Order aging shows how long specific orders remain open, which cannot be inferred from the aggregate queue total.
  7. exception queue Turn 14. The exception queue groups unresolved problems for the appropriate follow-up rather than treating all remaining orders as identical work.
  8. shipment confirmation Turn 16. Shipment confirmation records the defined dispatch event, not whether a promised arrival or timing requirement was met.
  9. partial fulfillment Turn 18. Partial fulfillment is not counted as a fully closed order in this case, so the completion rule must remain explicit.
  10. warehouse management system Turn 20. The warehouse management system provides operational records to reconcile against the stated arrivals and completion events.
Read the complete conversation

Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The backlog is 130, not 80

Fictional fulfillment shift: 300 orders were open at the start, 220 shipped, and 50 new orders arrived; there were no other order-status changes.

  1. Fulfillment supervisor: We shipped two hundred twenty orders from a starting backlog of three hundred.
  2. Operations manager: Then eighty orders remain open at the end of the shift, correct?
  3. Fulfillment supervisor: Fifty new orders also arrived, so the closing backlog is one hundred thirty.
  4. Operations manager: Thank you. I subtracted shipments but missed the new work entering the queue.
  5. Fulfillment supervisor: The complete flow is three hundred plus fifty, minus two hundred twenty, with no other changes.
  6. Operations manager: Does the shipment count tell us the pick rate for the team?
  7. Fulfillment supervisor: Not by itself. We need the picking measure and time basis before making that calculation.
  8. Operations manager: I'll report the verified closing backlog and keep any productivity interpretation separate.
  9. Fulfillment supervisor: I'll also identify which open orders need review without assuming all one hundred thirty are late.
  10. Operations manager: Good. The status update should distinguish unfinished orders from confirmed service failures and completed shipments.
Picked is not shipped

Separate fictional warehouse update: 75 orders are picked, 60 of those are packed, and none have confirmed carrier handover yet.

  1. Warehouse coordinator: Seventy-five orders are picked, and sixty of those are packed. Carrier handover isn't confirmed yet.
  2. Customer operations lead: Can I tell customers that all seventy-five have shipped because picking is complete?
  3. Warehouse coordinator: No. Picking, packing, and shipping are separate stages, and handover remains unconfirmed.
  4. Customer operations lead: Then fifteen picked orders still need packing, while sixty are further along but not confirmed shipped?
  5. Warehouse coordinator: Correct. The stages are nested counts here, so don't add them together as separate orders.
  6. Customer operations lead: Would a high pick rate justify promising that the remaining work will finish within an hour?
  7. Warehouse coordinator: Not without checking the current capacity and handover schedule. That completion time isn't supplied.
  8. Customer operations lead: I'll give a stage-based update and keep the delivery message conditional on verified dispatch.
  9. Warehouse coordinator: I'll request the next packing and carrier-status checkpoint from the responsible leads.
  10. Customer operations lead: Thanks. We'll communicate completed work accurately without turning preparation progress into a shipment confirmation.
Cycle counting covers only the checked locations

Separate fictional warehouse exercise: a cycle count covered 12 of 20 planned locations; eight remain uncounted, and reconciliation of the counted locations is pending.

  1. Inventory supervisor: We've counted twelve of the twenty planned locations. Eight remain, and reconciliation is still pending.
  2. Warehouse manager: Can I mark the inventory check complete because more than half the locations are done?
  3. Inventory supervisor: No. The count scope and reconciliation work are not complete, even though field counting has progressed.
  4. Warehouse manager: Do the eight uncounted locations indicate eight missing items in the backlog?
  5. Inventory supervisor: No. Uncounted locations are unfinished work, not evidence of missing stock.
  6. Warehouse manager: I'll separate locations counted, locations pending, and reconciliation status in the update.
  7. Inventory supervisor: Please also avoid using this partial count as proof that the new slotting plan is accurate.
  8. Warehouse manager: That comparison would need its own evidence. What can you confirm at the next checkpoint?
  9. Inventory supervisor: I'll provide the verified counting progress and any reconciled findings, keeping unresolved differences separate.
  10. Warehouse manager: Agreed. We'll update fulfillment planning from confirmed inventory information rather than treat partial coverage as a completed audit.

Use applicable law, governing contracts, current trade rules, authorized decisions, and qualified review. US customs examples are identified; requirements differ by jurisdiction.
Lesson 04: Warehousing and Fulfillment
Original fictional case: Warehouse lead Pavel and customer-operations manager Zoe review a shift that began with 300 open orders. Fifty new orders arrived during the shift, and 220 orders were fully shipped and closed. There were no cancellations, reopenings, or partial closures. The data do not identify which shipped orders came from the opening queue. A report says 80 remain and the oldest orders are cleared. Pavel and Zoe must correct the balance and separate the known throughput from the unsupported age and service claims.
Language workshop: Shipments and the closing queue
Communication goal: Reconcile a changing order queue, distinguish gross completions from net reduction, and avoid unsupported claims about order age or service.
Language guidance: 300 opening orders plus 50 arrivals minus 220 completed orders equals 130 closing orders. Gross completions are 220; the net queue reduction is 170. Ignoring arrivals produces the incorrect balance of 80.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We began the shift with 300 open orders. / Fifty new orders entered during the shift. / Two hundred twenty orders were fully shipped and closed. / The closing balance is 130 open orders. / We completed 220 orders, but the queue fell by 170. / Eighty omits the 50 new arrivals. / We do not know which completed orders came from the opening queue. / The total does not prove the oldest orders were cleared. / Show the remaining orders by age and due date. / Shipped volume does not by itself establish on-time delivery. / Do not mix orders, order lines, units, and cartons. / This report counts only fully closed orders. / Identify unresolved stock, documentation, or address issues separately. / Match the completion events to the warehouse record. / The next-shift plan needs current capacity and priority information. / Present arrivals, completions, ending balance, and remaining exceptions together.
Vocabulary: order backlog: Orders still awaiting the defined fulfillment completion. / opening balance: The quantity at the start of the reporting period. / closing balance: The quantity at the end after all relevant movements. / order inflow: New orders entering the queue during a period. / gross completions: Total completed orders before accounting for new arrivals or other queue changes. / net reduction: The decrease in the total balance after additions and removals. / fulfillment cycle: The steps from accepted order through the defined delivery or completion point. / order release: Authorization to begin warehouse processing for an order. / wave picking: Grouping orders into planned picking batches or time windows. / pick face: The location from which items are selected for orders. / pick accuracy: The correctness of selected items and quantities under the defined measure. / short pick: A pick that cannot supply the full requested quantity. / packing verification: Checking packed items against order and shipping requirements. / shipment confirmation: A recorded confirmation of the defined dispatch event. / partial fulfillment: Completion of only part of an order. / order aging: The time orders have remained open under the defined starting point. / due-date priority: Ranking work by agreed completion or delivery deadlines. / cutoff time: The latest time for inclusion in a specified processing or dispatch cycle. / warehouse management system: Software managing warehouse inventory and operations, abbreviated WMS. / scan event: A recorded identification action at a process step. / exception queue: Work set aside for problems requiring resolution. / dock capacity: The available capability for loading, unloading, and related dock work. / labor capacity: The work volume supported by available personnel under stated conditions. / order cohort: A defined group of orders, such as those open at the start of a shift.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 and repeat Zoe's corrected balance: 300 plus 50 minus 220 equals 130. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Stress the phrases that withhold claims about the oldest orders and on-time delivery. Complete and check the transfer. Say 50 remaining and 80 completed, then read the line leaving order ages unknown.
Partner: Pavel: Warehouse lead / Zoe: Customer-operations manager
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A shift starts with 100 open orders, receives 30, and fully closes 80. There are no other changes, and shipped-order ages are not supplied.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Zoe: I will change that heading too. We are counting fully closed orders, not cartons, individual units, or customer deliveries.
Pavel: Correct. This count excludes partial fulfillment. Shipping one carton from an unfinished order does not make that order one of our two hundred twenty closures.
Zoe: I have corrected eighty to one hundred thirty, kept the completed count, and removed the oldest-cleared and on-time-delivery claims. Please check the underlying events.
Pavel: I will reconcile them against the warehouse management system. Customer service will receive the corrected balance and the age and exception details, with any unresolved differences identified.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

05Freight, Routing, and Carrier DelaysWednesday is still an estimate+

Your goal

Give a useful delay update while separating a carrier estimate, confirmed transport events, appointment timing, and an authorized contingency.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Wednesday is still an estimate

Logistics coordinator Nikhil and customer planner Dana review a fictional shipment. The carrier currently estimates warehouse arrival on Wednesday, but departure has not been confirmed. Dana needs the goods for a Wednesday 14:00 production start and asks for a guarantee. An expedited route is only being assessed; its capacity, extra cost, and arrival timing are unconfirmed. Nikhil has arranged a carrier status update for Tuesday at 16:00. He must state what is known without treating that update or the alternative route as a delivery promise.

Nikhil: Logistics coordinator / Dana: Customer planner

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the current arrival status?

Answer and explanation

C. Estimated Wednesday warehouse arrival, with departure unconfirmed

The carrier supplies an estimate, while a key preceding movement has not been confirmed.

2. What does Tuesday 16:00 represent?

Answer and explanation

D. A carrier status update

The appointment concerns information, not the physical shipment event.

3. What is known about the expedited route?

Answer and explanation

D. It is being assessed; capacity, cost, and timing are unconfirmed.

The alternative remains a proposal with multiple unresolved conditions.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

An ETA is an estimate for a stated event and place. It is not proof of departure or a guarantee that goods will be usable for a production start. A warehouse appointment and a confirmed arrival are also different events.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
estimated time of arrival
A forecast arrival time for a specified destination, abbreviated ETA.qualify the estimated time of arrival
estimated time of departure
A forecast departure time from a specified location, abbreviated ETD.update the estimated time of departure
actual departure
The confirmed time the shipment left the stated location.confirm actual departure
actual arrival
The confirmed time the shipment reached the stated point.record actual arrival
carrier
The organization transporting goods under the relevant arrangement.request a carrier update
freight forwarder
A party arranging transport and related logistics services.coordinate with the freight forwarder
consignor
The party sending goods under the transport record.identify the consignor
consignee
The party named to receive goods under the transport record.confirm the consignee
bill of lading
A transport document evidencing receipt and carriage terms, with functions varying by type.review the bill of lading
air waybill
A nonnegotiable document used for air-cargo carriage.verify the air waybill
transport milestone
A defined event used to track shipment progress.confirm the transport milestone
tracking exception
A reported departure from the expected shipment process.investigate a tracking exception
transshipment
Transfer of goods between transport services during a journey.assess transshipment risk
routing
The planned path and transport connections for a shipment.review the routing
last mile
The final transport leg to the delivery destination.coordinate the last mile
delivery appointment
An agreed receiving time slot at the destination.confirm the delivery appointment
proof of delivery
Evidence documenting the defined delivery event.obtain proof of delivery
expedited freight
Transport arranged to reduce delivery time under specified conditions.assess expedited freight
premium freight cost
Additional transport cost for a higher-cost or urgent service.authorize premium freight cost
capacity booking
A reservation of transport space under the applicable terms.confirm the capacity booking
demurrage
A charge for specified excess terminal or port use under applicable terms.review demurrage terms
detention
A charge for specified extended equipment use under applicable terms.check detention exposure
Incoterms rule
An ICC trade rule allocating specified delivery obligations, costs, and risks.identify the agreed Incoterms rule
named place
The specific location stated with the relevant trade or delivery term.specify the named place

An expedited option needs capacity, timing, cost, and authorization before it becomes a plan. Incoterms rules allocate specified obligations, costs, and risks; they do not prove physical movement or themselves guarantee an arrival time. Read the actual contract and named place.

C Notice the language

Be useful without false precision

A customer asking for certainty often needs a planning decision. State the latest estimate, the missing milestone, the consequence for the customer, and the next update. Avoid filling the gap with a precise arrival time that no confirmed movement or service commitment supports.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State the latest estimate
The carrier currently estimates warehouse arrival on Wednesday.
Name the missing event
Departure has not yet been confirmed.
Avoid an unsupported guarantee
I cannot confirm arrival before your 14:00 production start from the available information.
Acknowledge the consequence
I understand that the uncertainty affects your production plan.
Clarify the destination
This estimate refers to your warehouse, not merely the destination terminal.
Request a milestone
Please provide the confirmed departure event and its source.
Separate appointment
A receiving slot is not proof that the vehicle will arrive in that slot.
Describe the alternative
We are assessing expedited routing, not reporting a completed booking.
Keep cost visible
Any premium cost needs the appropriate authorization.
Qualify capacity
Space must be confirmed before we rely on the alternative.
Set the communication
The next carrier status update is Tuesday at 16:00.
Preserve update discipline
I will update you then even if the arrival remains uncertain.
Do not relabel
A forecast departure is not actual departure.
Separate trade terms
The agreed trade rule does not itself guarantee Wednesday arrival.
Report changes clearly
State which milestone, estimate, or condition has changed.
Close with the planning basis
Use Wednesday as the current estimate, with the departure dependency explicit.
6 language and precision notes
Estimated
A forecast based on current information, not a confirmed event.
Actual
A recorded occurrence that needs a clear source and location.
Guaranteed
Requires an actual applicable commitment; do not infer it from an ETA.
Booked
Specify whether capacity is reserved or a request merely submitted.
Arrived
Name the terminal, warehouse, or other location.
Delivered
Identify the contractual or operational event rather than assume every arrival is delivery.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which customer update is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. Warehouse arrival is estimated Wednesday; departure remains unconfirmed; next update Tuesday 16:00.

The update preserves the carrier estimate, the missing milestone, and the separate communication commitment.

2. The system shows ETD Tuesday 10:00 but no departure event. Which wording preserves the distinction?

Answer and explanation

A. Departure is estimated for Tuesday 10:00; actual departure is unconfirmed.

ETD is an estimated departure time. A populated estimate field is not evidence of completed movement or a receiving appointment.

3. What is needed before relying on the expedited alternative?

Answer and explanation

C. Confirmed capacity, supported timing, cost, and the required authorization

The alternative's practical conditions and approval remain unresolved in the brief.

4. Which statement about an agreed Incoterms rule is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. It allocates specified responsibilities, costs, and risks but does not itself prove arrival or guarantee the ETA.

Trade-rule obligations and physical shipment status are different questions that require different evidence.

Check explanations in the book, page 93 ↗

E Conversations

Wednesday is still an estimate

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Nikhil: Logistics coordinator / Dana: Customer planner

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • Incoterms rule
  • premium freight cost
  • actual departure
  • proof of delivery
  • transport milestone
  • last mile
  • estimated time of arrival
  • delivery appointment
  • capacity booking
  • expedited freight
  1. Dana
    Our production starts Wednesday at fourteen hundred. The carrier estimate says Wednesday, so can you guarantee that the shipment will reach our warehouse before then?
  2. Nikhil
    The current 1 is Wednesday, but no time before fourteen hundred is confirmed. Departure itself has not been confirmed, which remains an important dependency.
  3. Dana
    Please put that in the update to production. They are planning around fourteen hundred, not simply some time on Wednesday.
  4. Nikhil
    I will state that 2 is still unconfirmed. A planned departure or an arrival estimate does not establish that the goods have already begun that leg.
  5. Dana
    Does the Wednesday estimate refer to our warehouse or just the destination terminal? That would make a significant difference to whether the goods can support production.
  6. Nikhil
    It refers to the warehouse, but the 3 remains part of the plan to verify. Arrival at an earlier terminal would not by itself complete that final movement.
  7. Dana
    We have a receiving slot available. I want to avoid treating that slot as confirmation that the carrier will reach us on schedule.
  8. Nikhil
    Correct. A 4 describes the receiving arrangement. It does not turn an uncertain transport estimate into a confirmed arrival event.
  9. Dana
    The customer-service note mentions a faster alternative. Is that already arranged, or are you still checking whether it could actually improve this shipment's timing?
  10. Nikhil
    We are assessing 5. Capacity, timing, and cost remain unconfirmed, so it would be misleading to describe the option as a booked recovery plan.
  11. Dana
    Please show any extra cost before someone commits us to it. A faster route might help, but we need the actual proposal and the appropriate approval.
  12. Nikhil
    Any 6 will be explicit. We should not assume that urgency supplies financial authorization or that a quoted service is already available for this shipment.
  13. Dana
    And is there actually space on that faster route? I do not want us to approve extra cost for a connection we cannot secure.
  14. Nikhil
    Exactly. We need the 7 and supported timing. I will separate a booking request from an actual confirmation in the update.
  15. Dana
    The order also names a trade term. Someone suggested that this term guarantees arrival on the estimated day, but I do not think those are the same issue.
  16. Nikhil
    They are not. An 8 allocates specified obligations, costs, and risks. It does not itself prove shipment movement or guarantee that the current estimate will be met.
  17. Dana
    What can you promise about the next communication? We need a reliable checkpoint even if the transport timing remains uncertain.
  18. Nikhil
    The carrier update is Tuesday at sixteen hundred. I will request the next confirmed 9 and tell you what changed, including if departure or the alternative still remains unresolved.
  19. Dana
    That gives us an honest planning basis. We will treat Wednesday as the current estimate, keep the production dependency visible, and wait for the confirmed movement update.
  20. Nikhil
    Once delivery occurs, we can obtain 10. Until then, I will distinguish estimates, bookings, confirmed events, and the next communication rather than combine them into a guarantee.

Answers and explanations
  1. estimated time of arrival Turn 2. The ETA is a forecast arrival at the warehouse, not a guarantee of a particular time.
  2. actual departure Turn 4. Actual departure requires confirmation that the goods left the stated point, which is missing in this case.
  3. last mile Turn 6. The last mile is the final transport leg to the warehouse and remains part of the estimated arrival plan.
  4. delivery appointment Turn 8. A delivery appointment reserves or agrees a receiving slot but does not prove the vehicle will arrive then.
  5. expedited freight Turn 10. Expedited freight is being considered as a faster option, but its practical conditions are not yet confirmed.
  6. premium freight cost Turn 12. Premium freight cost is the additional expense of the proposed service and needs the required authorization.
  7. capacity booking Turn 14. A capacity booking confirms reserved space under its terms, unlike an unconfirmed routing suggestion.
  8. Incoterms rule Turn 16. An Incoterms rule allocates specified obligations, costs, and risks; it does not itself guarantee an estimated arrival date.
  9. transport milestone Turn 18. A transport milestone is a defined shipment event whose confirmation can improve the status assessment.
  10. proof of delivery Turn 20. Proof of delivery documents the actual defined delivery event, unlike an estimate or appointment.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Dana: Our production starts Wednesday at fourteen hundred. The carrier estimate says Wednesday, so can you guarantee that the shipment will reach our warehouse before then?
  2. Nikhil: The current estimated time of arrival is Wednesday, but no time before fourteen hundred is confirmed. Departure itself has not been confirmed, which remains an important dependency.
  3. Dana: Please put that in the update to production. They are planning around fourteen hundred, not simply some time on Wednesday.
  4. Nikhil: I will state that actual departure is still unconfirmed. A planned departure or an arrival estimate does not establish that the goods have already begun that leg.
  5. Dana: Does the Wednesday estimate refer to our warehouse or just the destination terminal? That would make a significant difference to whether the goods can support production.
  6. Nikhil: It refers to the warehouse, but the last mile remains part of the plan to verify. Arrival at an earlier terminal would not by itself complete that final movement.
  7. Dana: We have a receiving slot available. I want to avoid treating that slot as confirmation that the carrier will reach us on schedule.
  8. Nikhil: Correct. A delivery appointment describes the receiving arrangement. It does not turn an uncertain transport estimate into a confirmed arrival event.
  9. Dana: The customer-service note mentions a faster alternative. Is that already arranged, or are you still checking whether it could actually improve this shipment's timing?
  10. Nikhil: We are assessing expedited freight. Capacity, timing, and cost remain unconfirmed, so it would be misleading to describe the option as a booked recovery plan.
  11. Dana: Please show any extra cost before someone commits us to it. A faster route might help, but we need the actual proposal and the appropriate approval.
  12. Nikhil: Any premium freight cost will be explicit. We should not assume that urgency supplies financial authorization or that a quoted service is already available for this shipment.
  13. Dana: And is there actually space on that faster route? I do not want us to approve extra cost for a connection we cannot secure.
  14. Nikhil: Exactly. We need the capacity booking and supported timing. I will separate a booking request from an actual confirmation in the update.
  15. Dana: The order also names a trade term. Someone suggested that this term guarantees arrival on the estimated day, but I do not think those are the same issue.
  16. Nikhil: They are not. An Incoterms rule allocates specified obligations, costs, and risks. It does not itself prove shipment movement or guarantee that the current estimate will be met.
